{
  "recorded_role": "user",
  "parentUuid": "7722abdf-016b-473f-b95b-5fc0baa6387a",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "promptId": "2dd7ac2f-f0b0-4010-aec4-71af2f9f9088",
  "type": "user",
  "uuid": "192f7d10-be5b-4fe7-858a-0188980c1f26",
  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
    "oldString": "all fail to widen the favorable region",
    "newString": "all fail to widen the intermediate-SC region",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\nThe oracle experiments provide a causal identification of the failure source.\nHolding all pipeline components fixed and varying only the constraint source, gold annotations yield $+14.22$\\,pp on FOLIO and $+11.50$\\,pp on ProofWriter (both $p < 0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:oracle}).\nThe best self-extraction result across all models and conditions captures only 24.1\\% of the FOLIO oracle ceiling.\nBecause the aggregation mechanism is identical in both conditions, the gap between oracle and self-extracted performance localizes entirely to extraction quality, not to solver limitations or the MAX-SAT formulation.\nThe aggregation mechanism is sound; the constraints are not.\n\nTrace correlation affects SICA through two channels. The direct channel limits voting diversity: the Condorcet Jury Theorem predicts that aggregation improves accuracy only when voters contribute independent information \\citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}, and Eff-$K < 2$ for many conditions (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}) confirms that same-model traces violate this requirement at $K = 12$. This channel explains the SC plateau. The indirect channel operates through constraint quality: correlated traces produce homogeneous constraints, yielding a discriminative rate of only 8.2\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:extraction-taxonomy}) and causing MAX-SAT to degenerate to weighted vote counting (\\S\\ref{sec:ablation}). This channel explains why SICA fails to surpass SC. The oracle result disambiguates the two: gold constraints applied to the same correlated traces yield $+14.22$\\,pp, proving that channel (b) is the proximal bottleneck. Trace correlation is the root cause, but its effect on SICA is mediated primarily through constraint homogenization rather than through voting diversity alone.\n\nThe extraction step maps natural language traces to FOL through the same model distribution, so constraints inherit the correlation structure of the traces that produced them.\nEven symbolic representations, which appear to introduce a qualitatively different signal (formal logic rather than natural language votes), collapse back into the shared model distribution once the extraction is performed by the same model.\nThe independence bottleneck is not about symbolic reasoning as a formalism; it is about self-extraction being unable to introduce information that the model does not already encode in its traces.\n\nNine interventions spanning six dimensions all fail to widen the favorable region (\\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\nContrastive prompting reduces the non-discriminative rate from 8.2\\% to 6.0\\%, but the newly discriminative constraints still mirror model bias.\nCross-model extraction fails in both directions: a weaker model extracting from stronger traces yields 0\\% discriminative constraints, while stronger models (14B+) extracting from weaker traces (7B) produce accuracy within $\\pm$1\\,pp of self-extraction.\nOnly 1 of 4 temperature settings produces a positive SICA effect; $T = 0.3$, $0.5$, and $1.0$ all yield negative or null effects ($\\Delta \\in [-1.47, 0.00]$\\,pp).\nEven in-context learning with gold FOL examples ($\\Delta = +0.50$\\,pp, n.s.) cannot break the confirmation bias pattern.\nThe consistent failure across orthogonal intervention strategies constitutes the strongest evidence that the bottleneck is structural, not an artifact of prompt design or solver configuration.\nIf any single dimension had succeeded, the bottleneck could be attributed to engineering; the uniform failure across all six implicates the self-extraction paradigm itself.\n\nPublished systems that report positive results on logical reasoning each bypass same-model self-extraction.\nDyLoS \\citep{xu2026dylos} composes specialized solvers, Aristotle \\citep{li2025aristotle} applies logic-complete decomposition, FoVer \\citep{wang2025fover} trains dedicated FOL verifiers, and Logic-LM \\citep{pan2023logiclm} routes problems to external provers.\nOur oracle result is consistent with their success: when constraints are discriminative (by whatever mechanism), symbolic aggregation works.\nThe critical difference is the source of constraints, not the aggregation procedure.\nThese systems trade the simplicity of self-extraction for architectures that introduce genuinely independent signal, exactly the property that CJT requires \\citep{ladha1992correlated}.\n\n\\paragraph{Statistical evidence.} No positive SICA effect reaches statistical significance at $\\alpha = 0.05$. The four-seed replication of Mistral-7B on FOLIO yields $p = 0.092$, $0.070$, $1.0$, and $1.0$; 2 of 4 seeds show marginal positive trends ($p < 0.10$) but none survives any correction for multiple comparisons. Effects range from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp (4-seed mean $+1.47$\\,pp). We report these as directional evidence, not significance claims. In contrast, the negative effects are significant: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($\\Delta = -2.83$\\,pp, $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($\\Delta = -2.50$\\,pp, $p = 0.011$) both reach $p < 0.05$, confirming that error amplification in the Type~C regime is a reliable phenomenon. Oracle controls remain highly significant ($p < 0.001$ on two datasets). The converging evidence for the independence bottleneck does not rest on any single $p$-value but on the pattern across analyses: positive direction in only 2/4 seeds, cross-model entropy decomposition showing regime-dependent mechanism (\\S\\ref{sec:entropy}), discriminative rate CI of [7.6, 8.8]\\%, and the oracle-extraction gap.\n\nFor practitioners, $C_0$ (multi-candidate rate) combined with SC accuracy forms a zero-cost deployment diagnostic before any constraint extraction is attempted.\nWhen $C_0 < 5\\%$, SICA reduces to SC by construction (degenerate).\nWhen SC exceeds ${\\sim}60\\%$, confirmation bias renders constraints non-discriminative (Type~A).\nWhen SC falls below ${\\sim}40\\%$, error amplification makes constraints actively harmful (Type~C).\nOnly a narrow band around 50--55\\% SC in one model-dataset condition shows positive effects, and even there, self-extraction captures barely a quarter of the oracle ceiling.\nThree paths forward trade simplicity for independence, as CJT predicts is necessary: trained extractors (data-driven), external provers (tool-augmented), and multi-model pipelines (ensemble-based).\nEach introduces a constraint source that is not a deterministic function of the reasoning model's own distribution.",

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/appendix.tex:267:All three intervals contain zero. The narrowest interval (Qwen-14B, [$-$1.12, $+$1.62]) rules out practically meaningful gains ($>$2pp) even in the most favorable case. The widest interval (LLaMA-8B FOLIO, [$-$5.88, $+$0.98]) skews negative, indicating that SICA may slightly hurt accuracy in this condition. These results corroborate the McNemar tests and power analysis: no condition provides evidence that symbolic constraint aggregation improves over majority voting.

_source line 7094 · 2026-05-19T11:21:15.665Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_015kxTx6Nbj3xetrCgujdrWE`</summary>

```text
67	\item \textbf{Regime-Dependent Effect.} Positive directional trends occur in a narrow accuracy band ($\sim$50--55\% SC) in one model on one highly formalizable dataset (2 of 4 seeds marginal at $p < 0.10$, none reaching $p < 0.05$; best $+3.43$\,pp, 4-seed mean $+1.47$\,pp). Above this range, confirmation bias renders constraints non-discriminative. Below it, error amplification degrades accuracy. Three failure modes (Type~A, B, C) explain the regime boundaries (\S\ref{sec:main-results}, \S\ref{sec:failure-modes}).
```

</details>
